Nowhere on this page (onboarding card :1190-1207, FAQ :2207-2211, or JSON-LD FAQ :217-221) does the $1,500 figure carry the Microsoft 365 qualifier or the Google Workspace/Apple carve-out. As written, a Google Workspace or Apple-fleet prospect would reasonably read $1,500 as their price too. |
| Calculator default — savings card | $4,667 saved/mo, $56,004 saved/yr, 73% less, In-house team total $31,250/mo (IT Tech $8,333 + Systems Engineer $10,833 + Network Engineer $12,083, all shown active) | pricing.html:1051(itTechCost), :1058(sysEngCost), :1065(netEngCost), :1069(hireCost), :1088-1089(savingsPerMonth), :1094-1095(savingsPerYear), :1101(savingsPct), :1115(savings-bar-fill width:27%) | CONTRADICTS — multiple ways. (1) These are hard-coded fallback values for the calculator's default state (10 users / Secure Growth), but assets/js/pricing.js immediately overwrites them on page load, and its own logic (roles array threshold at pricing.js:23-27, roleActive() at :90-92, renderRole() at :180-198) means Systems Engineer (threshold 30) and Network Engineer (threshold 75) are **inactive** at 10 users — they should render as "not yet needed at 10 endpoints" with a "—" cost, not $10,833 and $12,083. The correct live output for this exact default state is: In-house total $8,333/mo, saved $6,583/mo, saved $78,996/yr, 79% less, bar width 21%. (2) Even taken as pure static copy, the numbers don't reconcile with each other: $31,250 (hireCost) − $1,750 (ghosxtCostCompare, pricing.html:1078) = $29,500, not the displayed $4,667 savings — the savings-card figures ($4,667/mo, $56,004/yr, 73%, bar 27%) imply a completely different, unstated "internal cost" baseline (~$6,417/mo) than the hireCost row two lines above it ($31,250/mo). A crawler or AI answer engine reading the raw HTML (without executing pricing.js) will index the inconsistent, inflated $31,250/mo in-house-cost figure and the internally-contradictory $4,667/$56,004/73% savings triplet as the page's stated numbers. |
| Managed SIEM | "Managed SIEM (24/7 Threat Monitoring)" included in Compliance & Continuity | pricing.html:745, :1682-1704; JSON-LD :170; visible FAQ :2215 | CONTRADICTS — see House-Rule Violations below. VERIFIED FACTS explicitly states "Do not claim SIEM or anything not listed," and SIEM is not on the capability list. |

### website-development.md
| Calculator default project / total price | Static markup + inline `<script>` (website-development.html:2210-2287) default to **Essential, $1,800** (the `checked` attribute is on the Essential radio, website-development.html:804, and the result card is pre-rendered at $1,800, :1074-1076/:1086-1088/:1100-1102). But the deferred external script assets/js/webdev.js forces the default to **Business Pro, $3,200** on load (state.projectType = 'business' at line 24, and init() at lines 204-206 explicitly re-checks the Business Pro radio and re-renders). Because assets/js/webdev.js runs after the inline script (deferred scripts execute after the parser reaches the inline blocks but before DOMContentLoaded), any visitor or JS-executing crawler ends up seeing the calculator jump from $1,800 to $3,200 immediately on load. | website-development.html:804, :2210-2287 (static/inline default) vs. assets/js/webdev.js:23-24, :204-206 (JS-forced default) | **CONTRADICTS** — internal inconsistency between the page's own static default and its own script's forced default; directly undercuts the "starting at $1,800" claim used in the title, meta description, and Twitter card (see below) |
| Service-schema service area (2nd JSON-LD block) | California, Salinas, Monterey, Santa Cruz, San Jose only — no Texas/Florida/Nevada/Arizona/Oregon/Washington, and only 4 of the 11 Central Coast cities (missing Watsonville, Gilroy, Hollister, Marina, Pacific Grove, Seaside) | JSON-LD Service areaServed, "ghosxt:extra-schema" block, website-development.html:349-370 | **CONTRADICTS** (internal inconsistency) — this second structured-data block describes the same "Website Development" service far more narrowly than the LocalBusiness schema, the hero copy, the FAQ, and the Local Areas/footer lists on the very same page. An AI answer engine reading only this Service entity could conclude web-dev service is limited to 4 Central Coast cities and California, contradicting the rest of the page |
| Content that exists only inside JS | Pass (with caveat) | All calculator numbers (project prices, add-on prices, monthly maintenance, and the default result-card total) exist as static text in the raw HTML, not only inside JS — a non-JS crawler sees the same $1,800/$3,200/$5,900+/$800/$550/$1,200/$950/$1,500/$300 figures as the JSON-LD. The caveat is behavioral, not textual: assets/js/webdev.js changes which package is *selected by default* after it runs (see the CONTRADICTS row above), so the number a JS-executing visitor/crawler ends up seeing as "the" price can differ from the static default. |

## Status of the known contradictions from the brief

1. Google review count shown as 24 / 25+ / 26 on different pages: RESOLVED in the repo. All 108 materialized occurrences across 55 files read "26 Google reviews" and are inside the marker windows that scripts/update-review-count.py rewrites from site-config.json. No 24 or 25+ stragglers remain. If the live site still shows mixed counts, the fix is a deploy, not an edit.
2. "Trusted by 30+ businesses" vs review counts: NOT FOUND in the repo. The current trust line is "trusted by businesses across Monterey County" (no number). The closest live equivalent is the blog author-bio boilerplate on ~115 posts claiming "built security programs for 40+ Central Coast businesses", which is unverified and needs Uli's confirmation or removal.
3. Salinas page mentions dental practices: CONFIRMED. salinas.html lines 609, 669, 777, 778, 829, 831. Dental also appears in 59 other files; worst cases are hipaa-it-compliance.html (9 occurrences including title, meta, H1, JSON-LD), healthcare-it-services.html (10 including a dental case example), it-help-aptos.html (8), and the blog post blog/hipaa-compliant-it-medical-dental-monterey-county.html whose entire premise targets dental.
4. Onboarding $1,500 with no non-Microsoft scope note: CONFIRMED on pricing.html (the $1,500 fee carries no Microsoft 365 default-scope caveat and no note that Google Workspace and Apple fleet onboarding is quoted separately).

## Source-of-truth data file design (for all numbers)

Extend the existing, working pattern (site-config.json plus the marker-driven scripts/update-review-count.py) instead of inventing a new one:

- File: site-config.json grows to hold every marketing number: google_review_count, google_rating, pricing (tiny_team_monthly 600, core_per_user 125, secure_growth_per_user 175, compliance_continuity_per_user 250, onboarding_tiny_team 1000, onboarding_5_to_15 1500, onboarding_scope note), incident_notification_hours 4, serving_since 2021 [VERIFY], insurance summary [VERIFY wording].
- Mechanism: a new scripts/update-site-numbers.py generalizing update-review-count.py: each number is wrapped in an HTML comment marker where it appears (the ghosxt:trust-reviews pattern), e.g. <!-- ghosxt:price-core -->$125<!-- /ghosxt:price-core -->, and the script rewrites every marked span from site-config.json. Dry run by default, --apply to write.
- Coverage: pricing.html, cybersecurity.html, 11 city hubs, 4 county pages, assets/js/pricing.js TIER_PRICES (script rewrites the JS constants too, then minify.py --refs regenerates the .min.js), llms.txt key-facts block, and the JSON-LD UnitPriceSpecification values.
- Blog posts quote prices conversationally; those get corrected once in Phase 4 and future drift is caught by a grep in the script's --check mode listing any dollar figure adjacent to tier names that does not match the config.
